# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド31・32（レッスン 901〜960）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校・中学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 901 --to 960`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 7 / info 17 → あと error 0 / warn 7 / info 13**（warn の のこりは ぜんぶ わざと: タイトル「It Was Invented by…」「I Respect Her Because…」「The Graph Shows…」と その スライド〈テストで きまって いる〉6、L938 teacherScript の「After a rain」〈アメリカ英語で ふつう〉1。info は 会話の「What is」・L954 の「toilet」〈べんきそのもの。アメリカでも ふつう〉・せつめいの「very, very」・L929 の Let's 4回〈AI の「Let's pretend」の ため〉）
- なおした 欄: **405**（パッチ 417 件。ブロックの きめごと 67・AI の さいしょの ひとこと 59・レビュアー 250・あとの そろえ 41）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で、HEAD から じゅんばんに あてなおした（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証（unknownSlideItems）・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあいを たしかめた（ALL OK）。かいわ あいての チェック（.run9-w3132/partner.ts）も ALL OK

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. イギリスふうの ほめことば: 「Brilliant!」→「Great job!」（L905・L907・L915・L924・L932・L948・L953）・「Great guides!」（L910）・「Fantastic!」（L946）、「Lovely!」→「Nice!」（L928 teacherScript）
2. エマ先生 じぶんの 語りの「Ms. Emma says / asks」→「I say / I ask」、日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」（L906・L908・L926・L943・L944・L946・L954・L955・L957。L908・L956 の のこりは グループが なおした）
3. エマ先生の あいさつ「Good morning, explorers!」→「Hi, explorers!」（L933・L941）、「Good evening, everyone!」→「Hello, everyone!」（L956）、さいごの「Good night, and see you!」→「See you!」（L932・L956）。物語の なかの「Tonight」や フェン・ハンフリーの Good morning は のこした
4. お手本の「A! Or, B!」→「Or B」（L904・L911・L913）、L911「Maybe, She was born ～」→「You could say, ～」
5. **AI の さいしょの ひとこと**: アプリは あいてを こどもの キャラ（ハル・リコ・キコ・ビリモ）から えらび、その かおと なまえで ひとことを 出す。なのに「I'm Professor Nib.」「I'm Fen.」「Tick, tock!」など べつの 人として 話して いた。goalJa の やくは のこし、「Let's pretend I'm Old Tock / Professor Nib / Stella Moss / Fen / Humphrey / Dr. Mira / the castle guide ～.」で ごっこあそびだと わかる ように した（59 レッスン。L940 は やくが ないので そのまま。L901 は Who are you? と きく ゴールなので「the keeper of this corridor」に。W25-30 と おなじ）。2つめの Let's は「Time to / We can」に、lint の info（What is・Please show me・very, very）も ついでに なおした
6. あとの そろえ（グループの 報告から。20-after.json）:
   - **L959 core**「Camels have humps so that they can live without food.」→「～ so that they can go a long time without food.」（ラクダは たべものなしでは 生きられない。L936 の せつめいとも あう。テストの 正規表現 /so that .*The graph shows / は そのまま とおる。表現・targetExpressions・recap・台本・会話・スライド・L960 の まえの かいの 文を いっしょに。日本語は「たべものが なくても ながく すごせるように」）
   - ニブ先生の 日本語名を「ニブ先生」に そろえた（字幕 5 か所が「ニブ はかせ」）、金の ギアを「ギア」に（L915・L920 の「はぐるま」。L901 は「ギア（はぐるま）」で しょうかい）、L915 TPR の「ときの ちず」→「タイムマップ」
   - L915 phonics「And ation. ～ Observe plus ation」→「And a, t, i, o, n. ～ Observe, observation. Ob-ser-VA-tion!」（TTS が ation を ことばとして よむ）
   - L937「Talk about you.」→「Talk about yourself.」（L912・L914・L918 と おなじ）

## 学習項目が かわった もの
- L959 の core 表現（上の 6）。テスト・src は かえて いない（テストは 正規表現で、この 文を そのまま もって いない）
- L936 vocab caravan の 日本語「ラクダの たいしょう」→「ラクダと いっしょに たびを する 人たちの れつ」（英語・ID は そのまま）
- ほかの 表現・ことば・TPR・フォニックスの ことば・タイトルは かえて いない

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・テストで きまって いる・あとで 決める）
- タイトルと スライドの「…」、会話の「…」
- L918 core「Before the train was built, ～」（railway の ほうが しぜんだが テストで きまって いる）、L925 core「There are two reasons.」
- 「tap」（L954・L955・L959 の ことば・TPR・core。アメリカでは faucet が ふつうだが tap water・turn off the tap も つうじる。学習項目なので のこした）
- L954「toilet」（べんきの いみ）、L938「After a rain」
- たんどくの 接頭辞・接尾辞（「Im means in」「Re means」「Ist means」「Al makes a noun」）。TTS で きいて たしかめたい
- 「Birimo says, I'm playing Fen!」の あとの「Fen laughs」（ごっこあそびと して よめる）
- 「To plus a verb gives the reason」（L938・L939。こども むけと して のこした）

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L901-910 (world 31, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 32 patches in 7 lessons; L903, L905 and L907 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "used to" starts in L902, "was/were built / invented / made / found / used" in L906 and later only (L903 "The telephone was a big improvement" and L904 "are made of" do not match the rule), and no new text adds an order-pinned form. Themes: (1) **Phonics spelling rules**: L901 taught "Add t, i, o, n" to invent / protect / collect / connect, which would give "inventtion"; the verbs already end in t, so the ending added is i, o, n (src/lib/curriculum/w31Phonics.ts already says "invent ＋ ion"). Fixed in the teacherScript, seg10 and tipJa. (2) **Letter groups and stress cues TTS misreads**: L902 "Make A strong" (read as "make a strong"), L904 "Say al softly" ("Al"), L908 "Add the and an s". (3) **Wording a child would not follow**: L906 "Read years two numbers at a time", L909 "Now is the twenty-first century" and "The number is one ahead", L910 "Haru guides the castle model" (you guide people, not things). (4) **Small consistency fixes**: L902 Riko's retold line dropped today's "used to"; L908 seg8 JA kept エマ先生は にっこり although the EN is "I say"; praise repeated in back-to-back segments (L904 "Super!" / "Super job!", L910 "Great guiding!" / "Great guides!"). All facts checked are right (Meiji 1868, Tokyo Olympics 1964, Bell 1876, Wright brothers 1903 and about twelve seconds, Swan and Edison improving the bulb, rope-pattern clay pots about 5,000 years old, the century = hundreds + 1 rule). Every new sentence has 13 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All touched lessons are 215-228 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L901 The Corridor of Time
- A: The stone stairs, Old Tock as a walking grandfather clock and the Time Map note are warm and natural. "Make up a fun name. ... I'm Captain Gear" is a good Your turn. Nothing sounds translated.
- B: Each + singular verb (opens, with an s) is explained clearly. The phonics rule is wrong: teacherScript "Add t, i, o, n, and say shun", seg10 "Add t, i, o, n to an action word" and tipJa "どうしに -tion を つけると" all say we add -tion to invent / protect / collect / connect, which would spell "inventtion". The verbs end in t; we add -ion (w31Phonics.ts: "invent ＋ ion").
- C: Reads well. "shun" is a real word and TTS reads it close to /ʃən/ (kept). "Tick, tock" is the keeper's call (kept).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "These verbs end in t. Add i, o, n, and t, i, o, n says shun."; seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Sound time! These action words end in t. Add i, o, n, and they become things. Say t, i, o, n softly, shun." with JA to match; tipJa -> "t で おわる どうしに -ion を つけると、-tion で おわる ..." (objective). testPoints[1] ("-tion（どうしを なまえの ことばに …）") names the ending only and is kept.

#### L902 People Used to Walk
- A: The dusty road, the rest house sign and "My legs hurt just thinking about it!" are natural. The starter's "young travelers" (plural) to one child is a pretend greeting (single flag, kept).
- B: Used to = many times in the past, not now, is right, and the allowed JA hint is in the teacherScript. seg7 retells Riko as "But they saw mountains and rivers every day." while her conversation line (and the lesson target) is "they used to see". The -ation tip ("add a, t, i, o, n") skips the silent-e drop in explore / imagine / prepare; it is a common teaching shortcut and the tipJa words are correct (kept).
- C: seg9 "Make A strong." is read by TTS as "make a strong". L905 already has "Make the A strong".
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) -> "But they used to see mountains and rivers every day." (A+B; JA 見て いたんだね already fits). seg9 -> "Make the A strong." (B+C).

#### L903 There Were No Phones
- A: Hana's letter to Grandma ("My little brother ate three!") is lovely and natural. Ms. Emma's "Let's write a short letter to Old Tock, too!" is fine.
- B: There were no ~, so ~ joins reason and result clearly (seg8). The -ment words need no spelling change, and the tip is right. tipJa "develop（そだてる）" is loose (w31Phonics.ts uses はってんさせる) but not wrong for a child (single flag, kept). seg7 gives Haru's "It took a week" line to Riko; it is a retelling and the JA matches the EN (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well; "ment" alone is easy for TTS.
- Decision: no change.

#### L904 Old Toys, New Toys
- A: The toy room, Birimo's top and "I like both! Kites are fun outside, and games are fun on rainy days." are natural. seg20 ends "Super!" and seg21 starts "Super job!" back to back.
- B: Used to + make their own toys is well built; "I like both" as a happy answer is a good point. seg10 "Say al softly" uses a bare letter group.
- C: TTS reads "al" as the name Al /æl/, not the weak /əl/. "Super! ... Super job!" sounds repetitive.
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Say a, l softly." (B+C; the line already says "Add a, l"). seg20 "Super!" -> "Nice!" with JA いいね！ (A+C).

#### L905 Then and Now
- A: The Then and Now poster ("fixed things instead of throwing them away. Both have good points!") is a good model presentation. The first gear clicks in, as the test needs.
- B: Used to ~, but now ~ is the right contrast; the word-family review is correct (celebrate -> celebration drops e, as w31Phonics.ts says). "Kiko talks about toys." has no model sentence, but the scene stays clear (single flag, kept).
- C: "Make the A strong, ay-shun" reads fine. Speed TPR is short and crisp.
- Decision: no change.

#### L906 It Was Built in 1600
- A: The castle sign and "Birimo wants to climb the walls" are fun. In the teacherScript Old Tock says "No, no!"; in the conversation Ms. Emma says it (both are fine in their own telling; single flag, kept).
- B: Was built = somebody built it, castle first, is explained well, and the allowed JA hint is there. Facts: Meiji 1868 and the Tokyo Olympics 1964 are right. seg9 "Read years two numbers at a time" is unclear (two digits; each part is one number); the teacherScript says "in two pairs".
- C: Years are written as words in say ("sixteen hundred"), good for TTS. seg9 "two numbers at a time" then "Eighteen, sixty-eight" makes the listener count numbers.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Read years in two parts." (A+B; JA 2けたずつ is kept).

#### L907 It Was Invented by…
- A: The Invention Room card is clear. seg17 "The flying bike was invented by me!" is a playful boast; "I invented it" is more usual, but a proud child does say "by me!" (single flag, kept).
- B: Facts are right and handled carefully: the light bulb "was improved by many inventors" (Swan and Edison), the telephone by Bell in 1876, the Wright brothers' first flight in 1903 lasting about twelve seconds. Improved = made better is right. seg10 "For the two thousands, say two thousand" is true for 2001-2009 (the tipJa says 2000ねんだいの はじめ); "two thousand twenty-four" is also heard in US English, so it is not wrong (kept).
- C: "nineteen oh three" is written as words, good for TTS. "In the 1800s" in the teacherScript reads correctly.
- Decision: no change.

#### L908 It Was Found Under the Sea
- A: The Silver Wave timeline (sank in the 1800s, missed in the 1960s, found in the 1990s, cleaned in the 2000s) is consistent, and the sailors are safe. "Add the and an s, and it means ten years." is hard to follow.
- B: By to say who did it is right. seg8 EN is "I say, Yes." but the JA still says エマ先生は にっこり。 (Ms. Emma narrates herself and does not smile in the EN). The decade / century rule is right.
- C: seg10 "Add the and an s" runs small words together. Decades are written as words in say ("the nineteen nineties").
- Decision: seg8 subtitleJa -> わたし「ええ。…」 (objective JA). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Sound time! Put the in front and an s at the end. Now it means ten years." with JA まえに the、うしろに s を つけると (A+C).

#### L909 Who Was It Made By?
- A: The clay pot room, the farmer's pieces and the puzzle are good. seg9 "Now is the twenty-first century." is not natural English. seg17 "This picture was made by me!" is the same proud-child "by me" as L907 (kept).
- B: Facts are right (rope-pattern clay pots about 5,000 years old; 20th century = 1900s, 19th = 1800s, 15th = 1400s). seg10 and seg19 "The number is one ahead" is unclear; the point is that the century number is one bigger than the hundreds.
- C: "Now is the twenty-first century" trips the reader. "5,000" in say is written as "five thousand" (good).
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "We live in the twenty-first century now." (A+C). seg10 and seg19 "one ahead" -> "one bigger" with JA かずが 1つ 大きく なる (A+B). tipJa "かずが 1つ ずれるのが ポイント" is kept (true in JA).

#### L910 History Museum Guide
- A: The museum day, the girl asking "How do you know?" and the soup card are natural. The teacherScript says "Haru guides the castle model. Kiko guides the old light bulbs. Birimo guides a piece of the Silver Wave." You guide people, not objects. seg20 "Great guiding!" is followed by "Great guides!" in seg21.
- B: Same "guides the castle model" flag. Was built / made / found / used are reviewed well; the second gear clicks in. "Please follow me." is a guide speaking to visitors (polite to adults; lint please-command is fine here).
- C: "Great guiding! ... Great guides!" sounds repetitive. "bong, bong" reads fine.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Haru talks about the castle model. Kiko talks about the old light bulbs. Birimo talks about a piece of the Silver Wave." (A+B). seg20 "Great guiding!" -> "Way to go!" with JA やったね！ (A+C); seg21 "Great guides!" is the block-wide fix and stays.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Phonics "add t, i, o, n" when the verb already ends in t**: check the other W31 -tion lessons (and reviews such as L910, L915, L920...) for "Add t, i, o, n" with verbs that end in t (invent, protect, collect, connect, construct, instruct...). Use "Add i, o, n" (w31Phonics.ts `how` already says "＋ ion"). For -ate verbs (celebrate, educate) "drop the e, add i, o, n".
- **Bare letter groups and stress cues for TTS**: "Make A strong" (no "the"), "Say al softly", and likely "ern", "ian", "struct", "port", "geo" later. Grep say for `\b(al|ian|ern|tion|ment)\b` alone and for "Make A " / "Say [a-z]{2,4} " and spell them as letters or put them inside a real word.
- **Back-to-back praise after the block-wide swap**: "Brilliant" -> "Great job!" / "Great guides!" can now sit next to another "Great ~!" or the same praise (L910 seg20/21). Check the answer segment against the wrap-up in L915, L924, L932, L948, L953.
- **Ms. Emma JA left as エマ先生 after "I say"**: L908 seg8 had エマ先生は にっこり (not caught by the エマ先生「 pattern). Grep subtitleJa for `エマ先生は` where the EN has "I say".
- Kept on purpose: L901 "shun"; L902 "young travelers" in the starter and the -ation e-drop shortcut; L903 develop（そだてる） and the seg7 speaker retelling; L905 "Kiko talks about toys."; L906 Old Tock vs Ms. Emma saying "No"; L907 and L909 "by me!" boasts and "For the two thousands, say two thousand"; all "…" in the L907 title and slide, "Tick, tock!", "Bye-bye!", "Please follow me."

### Run 9 review notes: L911-920 (world 31, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Or B", "You could say,", "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 41 patches across all 10 lessons, so every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is still clean: "was born" first appears in L911, "had already" in L917 and "what if" in L919. No new "had", "what if" or "I respect" forms were added before their start lessons. Themes: (1) **Grammar "point" segments that do not teach the form**. L911 seg8 said "Was born is always in the past" and now says "We say was born, not just born". L912 seg8 was a fragment ("When she was ten, then what she did."). L913 seg8 and seg20 said so shows "what happened next"; so shows the result. L919 seg8 never named were / would. (2) **Unfinished definition "The ending ian / ist means a person who."** (L916, L917, L919 seg9), now "...a person who does something." to match the teacherScripts. (3) **"Talk about you" / "Say it about you" / "Think about you"** (L912, L914, L918 "Your turn!"), now "...yourself". (4) **A wrong speaker**: in L917 seg7, Kiko seemed to say "I'm playing the usher"; it now has "And I say,". (5) **Fact and phonics**: the L918 museum card said "between Shimbashi and Yokohama" and then "the two cities", so it now says "Shimbashi in Tokyo". The L916 tipJa said every -ian word sounds イアン, but musician and magician end in -cian (シャン), as the L918 tipJa says. (6) **Japanese**: L914 ずるい for "That's not fair!" (said about unfair treatment of girls) -> おかしいよ. L919 seg2 translated "lights" as でんき, the same word as electricity. L911 つくりの ひと -> つくりばなしの ひと. L912 seg12 had a stray space. Every new sentence has 13 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK: all lessons run 217-228 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L911 She Was Born in a Small Village
- A: The Biography Door, the giant picture book and Stella's village ("one road, one shop, and many sheep") are lovely, and "made up" is in the teacherScript (tested). seg8 "Was born is always in the past." is not what a teacher says as a grammar point.
- B: Same seg8 flag. The point should show the form (was + born), because Japanese learners say "I born in ~". The JA つくりの ひと is not natural Japanese. seg2 defines a biography as "a real person's life" while Stella is made up, but the teacherScript and conversation say clearly that her story is made up (kept). The JA でんき (biography) is the same word as でんき (electricity) in L919, but the vocab JA explains it (note only).
- C: seg7 "Kiko smiles. There were no city lights..." has no "says". It still reads well because the line is true as narration too (single flag, kept). "so she could see so many stars" repeats so (single flag, kept; it is a conversation line).
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Here's the point. We say was born, not just born. Stella is made up, but her story feels real." and JA -> born だけ では なく「was born」と いうよ。ステラは つくりばなしの ひと だけど、… (A+B, objective JA).

#### L912 When She Was Ten
- A: The paper-tube telescope, Grandma's old glasses and Birimo eating clocks ("Please don't eat me!") work well. seg16 "Talk about you." is not natural; a teacher says "Talk about yourself." "Super work!" is a little unusual but fine (single flag, kept).
- B: seg8 "When she was ten, then what she did. Both in the past." is a fragment, so the order (when-clause, then main clause, both past) is not clear. The fact/opinion check ("It was beautiful.") is good. "old glasses lenses" is a bit heavy but it is a learning expression (kept). The seg12 JA has a stray space in かため を.
- C: seg8 is hard to read aloud; the fragment has no verb.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Here's the point. First say when, like When she was ten. Then say what she did. Both are in the past." (A+B+C; the JA already says this). seg16 -> "Your turn! Talk about yourself." (A+B). seg12 JA かためを (objective JA).

#### L913 She Became a Scientist
- A: The library, candlelight and the observatory on the hill are natural. seg8 "Poor, so she borrowed books." is a fragment.
- B: seg8 says so "shows what happened next". so shows a result (the JA already says だから どう なったか), and seg20 repeats "what happened next". The ages and years add up (born 1890, city at 18, astronomer in 1920). The de- / con- explanations are right.
- C: seg9 "Add de, and it turns around." TTS may read the bare "de" oddly (single flag, kept; see block-wide notes). seg4 "people read by candlelight" is past-tense read after "Long ago" (kept).
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Here's the point. So shows the result. Her family was poor, so she borrowed books." (A+B). seg20 -> "...and use so to show the result." with JA so で「だから どう なったか」を いえるね (A+B).

#### L914 She Never Gave Up
- A: The dark page, the broken telescope and Birimo's cookie joke are good. seg17 "Say it about you." should be "Say it about yourself."
- B: Riko's "That's not fair!" (about people saying girls can't be scientists) is translated そんなの ずるい！. ずるい means sly or cheating; the natural reply is そんなの おかしいよ！. The re- = again tip is right.
- C: seg7 "Riko nods. Many people said no..." and the teacherScript "Riko frowns. That's not fair!" have no "says", but both read fine (single flag, kept). seg9 "Re means again." has the bare "re" (single flag, kept).
- Decision: conversation line 0 JA and seg6 JA ずるい -> おかしいよ (objective JA). seg17 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Your turn! Say it about yourself." (A+B).

#### L915 A Life in Five Sentences
- A: Kiko's five-sentence card and Moss's Comet close the biography nicely. The third gear is in the teacherScript and conversation (tested). seg5 "Sentence. Words together with a full idea." is not a natural definition.
- B: Same seg5 flag; the JA says ことばの あつまり (a group of words). Born -> became -> famous for -> year -> later is clear. The years match L911-913 (1890, 1920, 1931). "We read years in two pairs" is right for 1964.
- C: seg10 "Obser-VA-tion" uses the house hyphen beat form (kept). "And ation" reads acceptably (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg5 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Sentence. A group of words with one full idea." (A+B).

#### L916 Timeline Talk
- A: Professor Nib's arrival and the Willow Hill timeline (1900, 1920, 1950, 1985) are consistent. seg17 "Tell your morning in order." is not natural English.
- B: seg9 "The ending ian means a person who." stops in the middle; the teacherScript says "a person who does something". The tipJa says -ian is always 「イアン」, but musician and magician end in -cian and sound シャン (the L918 tipJa already says -cian sounds シャン). The stress spell-outs (HIS-to-ry / his-TO-ri-an, MU-sic / mu-SI-cian) are right. The JA calls him ニブ先生 in the conversation and TPR but ニブ はかせ in segments (block-wide note, not changed).
- C: seg9 trails off after "who." seg17 is awkward to say.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "...means a person who does something." (A+B+C). phonics.tipJa adds musician・magician の -cian は「シャン」と きこえるよ。 (objective). seg17 -> "Your turn! Tell me about your morning in order." (A+C).

#### L917 It Had Already Started
- A: The late arrival, the usher and the Time Theater program are natural, and "An usher" is correct. In seg7, "Kiko says, Sorry we're late. I'm playing the usher. Don't worry..." sounds as if Kiko plays the usher. In the conversation, Ms. Emma plays the usher.
- B: Same seg7 flag. The past perfect explanation is right: "We arrived in the past. The show started even earlier", and the first event gets had. seg9 has the same unfinished "a person who." seg4 "A small paper about the show" is a little Japanese-English (single flag, kept).
- C: seg7 needs a speaker change for TTS. seg9 trails off.
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Kiko says, Sorry we're late. And I say, I'm playing the usher. Don't worry. Come in quietly. ..." (A+B+C; the JA already has わたしは). seg9 -> "...a person who does something." (A+B+C).

#### L918 Before and After
- A: The model railway and Professor Nib on the train roof are fun. seg17 "Think about you." is not natural.
- B: Fact: the first railway (1872, Shimbashi-Yokohama) is right, but "It ran between Shimbashi and Yokohama" followed by "between the two cities" makes Shimbashi a city. The core "Before the train was built" would more naturally be "Before the railway was built". It is the regex-pinned core in tests/curriculum.test.ts line 3378, so I only report it. The -cian = shun note is right.
- C: Reads well. "Choo, choo" and "Clip-clop" are fine for TTS.
- Decision: teacherScript card -> "It ran between Shimbashi in Tokyo and Yokohama." (objective fact). seg17 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Your turn! Talk about yourself." with JA じぶんの ことを いって みて (A+B).

#### L919 What If?
- A: The dark room, the candle and Birimo's melting ice cream are natural. "you cook bread over a fire" could be "toast bread" (single flag, kept).
- B: The second conditional sentences are correct. seg8 "It's not real. We're just imagining!" never says which form shows that (were + would). The seg2 JA translates "lights" as でんき, so the line reads "the electricity that runs electricity". seg9 has the same unfinished "a person who."
- C: seg9 trails off. The rest reads well.
- Decision: seg2 JA でんき -> あかり (objective JA). seg8 adds "So we say were and would." with JA だから were と would を つかうよ。 (A+B). seg9 -> "...a person who does something." (A+B+C).

#### L920 Our Town's Timeline
- A: The town timeline (1950 festival, 1964 Tokyo Olympics on the town hall TV, 1972 library, 2001 station) and Haru's grandpa are warm. The fourth gear is there ("Four gears down, two more to go"). goalJa keeps ほんとうの (tested). seg18 "The new station opened in which year?" is an echo-question order a teacher would not use for a quiz.
- B: Same flag; "When did the new station open?" is the normal question. "twenty ten" for 2010 is correct US reading. "two hundred one" is a good distractor.
- C: "And al. Propose plus al" reads acceptably (the name-like "al" is close to the sound; single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg18 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Quiz time! When did the new station open? Two, zero, zero, one. How do we say it? ..." with JA できたのは いつ？ (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"The ending ian / ist means a person who."**: seen in L916, L917 and L919 seg9 and fixed here. Please grep W31 (L921-930 reviews) for `means a person who\.`.
- **"Talk about you." / "Say it about you." / "Think about you."** in "Your turn!" segments: fixed in L912, L914 and L918. Grep W31-32 for `about you\.` in say / subtitleEn.
- **Grammar point segments that only say "both in the past" or "it's not real"**: check L921-930 seg8 for a point that names the form (I respect ~ because, I'd like to talk about).
- **Professor Nib's JA name**: the conversation, TPR, sceneJa and goalJa use ニブ先生, but the teacher segments use ニブ はかせ. "Professor" is はかせ, so one name across W31 would be better. I did not change it here because it spans many lessons and the test title for L916 uses ニブ先生 only in a describe string. The JA for the Time Map and gear also mixes ときの ちず / タイムマップ and ギア / はぐるま.
- **Bare prefixes for TTS**: "Add de", "Re means again", "Ist means", "ation", "Al makes a noun". These are single flags (C). If TTS testing shows they come out wrong, use "D-E" style spell-outs or put them inside a word ("de, as in destroy").
- **Core "Before the train was built"** (L918) is test-pinned. "Before the railway was built" would be more natural; this is an owner decision.
- Kept on purpose: "X smiles / nods / frowns / sighs. <speech>" without "says" (L911 seg7, L914 seg7 and teacherScript, L917 and L920 teacherScripts; each line also reads as true narration); "so she could see so many stars"; "Super work!"; "old glasses lenses" (expression); "A small paper about the show"; "cook bread over a fire"; the biography definition "a real person's life" next to made-up Stella; "Bye-bye!"; the hyphen beat forms (Obser-VA-tion, his-TO-ri-an, mu-SI-cian).

### Run 9 review notes: L921-930 (world 31, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 64 patches in 8 lessons; L922 and L928 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "I respect" first appears in L922 and "I'd like to talk about" in L925, and no new passive, past perfect or "what if" form is added anywhere. Themes: (1) **Missing speaker tags in segments** (L921 seg7, L925 seg6 and seg8, L926 seg7, L927 seg6 and seg7, L929 seg7, L930 seg7): a quoted line came straight after narration ("Haru presses the button. Buzz! It was in nineteen oh three."), so TTS reads it as the narrator's words. I added "He asks," / "and says," the way the teacherScript does. (2) **Story and fact errors**: L925 seg7 gave Birimo's two reasons about Old Tock to Haru's grandpa; in the L927 teacherScript Kiko answers the moon question, but in the conversation and seg8 Riko does (now Kiko answers the telephone question, Riko the moon question, Birimo the bonus question, so the 2-2 tie adds up); "When were the Tokyo Olympics held? 1964" ignores the 2021 Games (now "the first Tokyo Olympics"); L930 defined a desert as "sandy" just before W32 (most deserts are not sand). (3) **Suffix wording for TTS**: "Invent plus ion", "Celebrate plus ion", "Explore plus ation", "a noun with tion" and "Ex, plo, RA, tion!" (L927, L929, L930). TTS reads "ion" as eye-on, and the target /shun/ ending gets lost. I used L901's own wording ("Say the end softly, shun"), L902's spelling ("a, t, i, o, n") and the hyphen beat form "Ex-plo-RA-tion". (4) **Vague rules**: "The number is one ahead" for centuries (L926 seg9, L930 seg9, seg19) is now "Nineteen is one more than eighteen" / "Twenty-one is one more than twenty"; L929 seg8 "Was built, things" is now "something people made". (5) **Small wording fixes**: frame names like "Ask, When did you? and Why did you?" (L924 seg1, L927 seg20), the L923 quiz "Where is a harbor for airplanes?" with place names as answers, the L926 "I never give up on my dream!" model, the L927 "Be the quiz host" model that asks about the child's own birthday party, and the double "Next" in the L930 wrap-up. Every new sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All touched lessons are 216-228 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L921 People Who Changed the World
- A: The Hall of Great People, the talking paintings and Birimo's "Twelve seconds is short, but it was a big start!" are warm and natural. seg7 "Haru sees two brothers with an airplane. What did they do?" has no "He asks," so the question sounds like the narrator's.
- B: The facts check out: Marie Curie was born in Poland in 1867 and was the first woman to win a Nobel Prize (two); Anne Sullivan taught Helen Keller words; the Wright brothers flew for about 12 seconds in 1903. The "was / did for people long ago" point is right, and "was born" is allowed here (from L911). The port = carry tip and im / ex are correct.
- C: seg7 runs the narration into Haru's question. "Im means in" (teacherScript, seg9): some TTS voices may read the bare "Im" as I'm (single flag, kept; see block-wide notes).
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) -> "...with an airplane. He asks, What did they do?" with JA 見つけて きくよ。「なにを したの？」 (A+C).

#### L922 I Respect Her Because…
- A: Respect cards are natural, and the fact / opinion pair works for a child (patient teacher = opinion, two Nobel Prizes = fact). "Old Tock asks Haru, Why do you respect Anne Sullivan?" comes before Haru has said so, but the conversation has the right order (single flag, kept).
- B: I respect ~ because ~ first appears here (tested). The respect / patient / brave definitions are clear. re = back and sup = under (sub) are fine roots for kids. seg8 "Who, then because, then the reason" is short but clear with the slide.
- C: Reads well. "Sup means under" comes out as /sʌp/, which is fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### L923 Everyday Heroes
- A: The empty frames where heroes appear and Birimo's frame showing Old Tock are lovely. The seg18 quiz "Where is a harbor for airplanes? A, a seaport. B, an airport." is odd because "Where" asks for a location.
- B: Same flag: the answer choices are kinds of places, so "Which one" fits. goalJa keeps ほんとうの (tested). port = harbor (airport, seaport, passport) is a separate meaning from carry, and the tip says so. "wrench" is US (JA スパナ is fine).
- C: Reads well. "Hello, hello!" is fine.
- Decision: seg18 (say, subtitleEn) "Where is" -> "Which one is", JA どこ -> どっち (A+B).

#### L924 Interview a Historical Figure
- A: Stella stepping out of the picture book, Kiko's interview notes and "Keep asking questions!" are natural. seg1 "Ask, When did you? and Why did you?" are not questions a child can say.
- B: The Stella details match L911-915 (age ten, glasses-lens telescope, "girls couldn't be scientists", the comet in 1931). The conversation has Birimo playing Stella while seg7 says "Stella says"; that is the role-play convention used across the block (single flag, kept). porter / heliport / spaceport are explained correctly.
- C: seg1 reads as two broken questions. "glasses lenses" is a little clunky but repeats L911-915 (kept for consistency).
- Decision: seg1 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Ask questions with When did you and Why did you. Then say, ..." (A+C).

#### L925 Hero Presentation
- A: The stage, Riko's Marie Curie talk and Old Tock's face turning pink are charming. seg6 has no speaker tags ("Who wants to go first? Riko takes a deep breath. Me! ..."), and seg8 "Everyone claps. That was a great presentation!" has none either.
- B: seg7 says Haru gives the two reasons "he is kind / he always shows us the way", but in the teacherScript and the conversation those are Birimo's reasons about Old Tock (a story contradiction). The core expression "There are two reasons." does not say what the reasons are for (no "He is my hero for two reasons"), but it is a learning item, so I kept it and report it here. I'd like to talk about first appears here (tested). goalJa keeps ほんとうの.
- C: seg6 and seg8 run quotes into the narration. "Ian means a person": TTS reads "Ian" as the name, which sounds close to /iən/ (kept).
- Decision: seg6 (say, subtitleEn) -> "I ask, Who wants to go first? Riko takes a deep breath. She says, Me! ..." with JA わたし「 (A+C). seg7 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa): Birimo comes before the two reasons (objective). seg8 "Everyone claps and says, ..." (A+C).

#### L926 A Biography to Read
- A: Professor Nib's library, the thin blue book and "her story is made up, but it is like the lives of many real scientists" are good. seg6 "Kiko reads. Her family was poor, so she studied with library books." is her summary, not reading (JA したんだね is commentary). seg7 "Haru thinks hard. Many people said..." has no tag. seg17 "I never give up on my dream!" sounds like a habit, not a promise.
- B: Same flags on seg6 and seg17. seg9 "The number is one ahead!" does not say which numbers. The biography matches L915 (born 1890, astronomer 1920, comet 1931, free star school). The "fact in the story" quiz is a nice touch.
- C: seg7 runs Haru's answer into the narration. "The nineteenth century" reads well.
- Decision: seg6 "Kiko reads." -> "Kiko says," with JA キコちゃん「 (A+B). seg7 "He says," (A+C). seg9 -> "Nineteen is one more than eighteen!" with JA 19は 18より 1つ 大きいね！ (A+B). seg17 -> "I will never give up on my dream!" (A+B).

#### L927 History Quiz Show
- A: The quiz hall, the buzzers and "Everyone wins today, because everyone learned something new" are lovely. seg6 and seg7 have no speaker tags. seg17 "Be the quiz host. ... When was your birthday party?" is not a history quiz question, and it asks about the child's own life. seg20 "You can ask, When was? and Who invented?" names frames as broken questions.
- B: The facts are right (Bell's telephone 1876, Japan's first railway 1872 Shimbashi-Yokohama, the moon in 1969, the Wright brothers in 1903), but "When were the Tokyo Olympics held? 1964" is misleading now that Tokyo also held the 2021 Games. The teacherScript has Kiko answer the moon question, while the conversation and seg8 have Riko do it. The fix (Kiko: telephone, Riko: moon, Birimo: bonus) also makes Team Sun 2 and Team Moon 2, so the tie adds up.
- C: "Invent plus ion" makes TTS say eye-on, and "a noun with tion" in the teacherScript is a bare letter group.
- Decision: teacherScript "the first Tokyo Olympics", the answer order, and "Invention. Invent becomes a noun. Say the end softly, shun." (objective fact / story; B+C for the suffix). seg6 "and says," and seg7 "Birimo says," with JA ビリモ「 (A+C). seg9 -> "Invent, invention. Say the end softly, shun." (B+C). seg17 -> "When was the first moon walk?" with JA はじめて ひとが つきを あるいたのは いつ？ (A+B). seg20 -> "You can ask questions with When was and Who invented." (A+C).

#### L928 My Hero Report
- A: Kiko's 80-word report (bakery, soup for sick neighbors) is warm and natural, and Haru noticing that 1950 is the festival year ties back to L920 nicely. Kiko's "Sure." followed by Riko's "Sure." in seg8 repeats a word, but both are natural answers (single flag, kept).
- B: "My hero is ~. She was born in ~. I respect her because ~. For example, ~." is well built, and the core regex is untouched. goalJa keeps ほんとうの. "Instructor. Struct means build, and an instructor is a person who teaches." does not show how build links to teach (single flag, kept). The report counts about 78 words, which is close to 80.
- C: Reads well. "Eighteen, fifty!" is clear.
- Decision: no change.

#### L929 Corridor of Time Review
- A: The goodbye walk past the old road, the castle, the clay pot and the Time Theater recaps the world well, and the details match L901-920 (castle about 400 years old, the pot 5,000 years old, the ship found under the sea, the late show). seg7 "Kiko points. This pot was made..." has no tag.
- B: seg8 "Used to, old habits. Was built, things. Had already, it happened first." The middle item tells a child nothing. "What did people use to do" is correctly spelled after did. The core regex (used to .* was built) is untouched.
- C: seg9 "Explore plus ation ... Ex, plo, RA, tion!" reads badly (bare "ation", comma syllables, "tion"). Same for "a noun with ation" in the teacherScript.
- Decision: seg7 "Kiko points and says," (A+C). seg8 -> "Was built, something people made." with JA ひとが つくった もの (A+B). seg9 -> "Explore, exploration. The strong beat is ra. Ex-plo-RA-tion!" with JA (B+C). teacherScript "with a, t, i, o, n" as in L902 (B+C).

#### L930 World 31 Grand Review
- A: The festival poster ("people in the past were children once, too") and the sunlight pouring through the last door are a lovely close. The teacherScript has "timelines.Haru" with no space. The seg20 wrap-up says "Next, go and talk with Riko! Next is World thirty-two..." with two Nexts. seg7 Birimo's line has no tag.
- B: seg5 "Desert. A big, dry, sandy place" is misleading for W32 (most of the Sahara is rock and gravel, and Antarctica is a desert); "dry place with very little rain" is the real definition. seg9 and seg19 "The number is one ahead" is vague. The speed TPR, the gear, the Desert of the Sun and 8 conversation lines meet the tests.
- C: "Celebrate plus ion" and "a noun with tion" have the same TTS problem as L927.
- Decision: teacherScript "timelines. Haru" (objective) and "Celebration. Celebrate becomes a noun. Say the end softly, shun." (B+C). seg5 -> "A big, dry place with very little rain." with JA ひろくて かわいた ばしょ (objective fact). seg7 "and says," (A+C). seg9 -> "Celebrate, celebration. Say the end softly, shun. ... Twenty-one is one more than twenty!" (B+C / A+B). seg19 -> "Twenty-one is one more than twenty." (A+B). seg20 "Next is World thirty-two" -> "Then, on to World thirty-two, the Desert of the Sun!" with JA それから …へ！ (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Missing speaker tags in segments**: grep W31-32 say lines for a narration sentence followed directly by a quoted line ("X points.", "X presses the button. Buzz!", "Everyone claps.", "X thinks hard.", "X swings ..."). Add "X says," / "and says," as the teacherScript does.
- **Suffix review wording for TTS**: "<verb> plus ion", "plus ation", "with tion / with ation", "Ex, plo, RA, tion!". L901-902 already use "Add t, i, o, n ... Say it softly, shun" and "Add a, t, i, o, n". I used those (and "Say the end softly, shun") in L927, L929 and L930. Other groups may have "plus ion / plus ation" in L905, L910, L915 and L920 review segments.
- **"The number is one ahead"** for centuries: fixed in L926 and L930. Grep W31 for `one ahead` (L909, L915 and L920 may have it) and say which numbers ("Nineteen is one more than eighteen!").
- **"Ask, When did you? and Why did you?"-style frame names** (L924, L927): check other goal and wrap-up segments for frames written as broken questions ("When was?", "Who invented?").
- **Desert = sandy**: check W32 definitions (L931 onward) for "sandy place" or "sand everywhere" in a general definition of desert.
- **Bare prefixes**: "Im means in" (L921) may be read as I'm by some TTS voices, "Sup means under" (L922) is fine, and "Ian / Ist means a person" (L925, L927-930) sound close enough. I kept them all as single flags; if the team wants to be safe, "I, M means in" follows the L901 letter style.
- **Learning item to consider (not changed)**: the L925 core "There are two reasons. First, he is kind. Second, ..." does not say what the reasons are for. "He is my hero for two reasons." would be clearer, but the expression is pinned in aiTask.targetExpressions, the slides and the recap.
- Kept on purpose: Birimo playing Stella / Old Tock while the characters are present (role-play convention); "glasses lenses" (matches L911-915); L922 Old Tock asking Haru why before Haru says it; L928 "Sure. ... Sure."; L928 instructor explanation; all "…" in titles and slides, "Tick, tock!", "Squeak!", "Bye-bye!" and the years written as words in say.

### Run 9 review notes: L931-940 (world 32, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 25 patches in 8 lessons; L931 and L938 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected (one vocab[].ja changed: L936 caravan). The grammar order is clean: "hardly ever" first appears in L931, "located" in L935, "so that" in L937 and "in order to" in L939; no new sentence adds a later form ("in order to" was avoided in the L937 rewrite). Themes: (1) **Suffix spelling rules stated wrongly**: "Add e, r to geography" (L932) and "Add i, c, and geography becomes..." (L934) both skip that the y drops; the L932 tipJa said geography＋er too. (2) **Invented respellings TTS will misread**: L933 "Jee oh", L936 "nay to na" / "Nay becomes na" (TTS says "nah", the wrong vowel; natural / national have the short a of cat), L939 "Say men strongly!" (reads as the word men) -> hyphen beat form en-vi-ron-MEN-tal. (3) **so that explained as a reason**: L937 teacherScript "means for this reason" ("for this reason" means "therefore") and seg8 "So that tells the reason"; now "tells us what something is for" / "So that tells us why." (the L940 wording), JA なんの ためか. (4) **Missing speaker tags** in L936 seg7 ("No, no." ran into Riko's question) and L940 teacherScript ("It's Humphrey! Yes, it's me!"). (5) Small items: L932 "Night time" -> "Nighttime" (US spelling), L935 "The Sahara is in the north of it", L936 caravan JA たいしょう. Facts checked and correct: under about 250 mm of rain, Antarctica largest desert, most of the Sahara rock and gravel, Sahara almost as big as the US, Atacama in Chile, Gobi in Mongolia and China, Amazon largest rainforest, camels rise back legs first, humps store fat, about 100 L at once (half a Japanese bathtub), fennec smallest fox, saguaro over 12 m and 150+ years, spines are leaves, emperor penguins huddle, polar bear black skin, Arctic fox white in winter. The check script passes with ALL OK; lessons are 220-239 s with 100% English and every slide item is valid.

#### L931 The Desert of the Sun
- A: Fen popping out of the sand, the guidebook page and the Sun Compass are warm and natural. "It gets less than about two hundred fifty millimeters" is a bit stacked but fine. seg17 "It hardly ever snows here. Say yours!" is a good model.
- B: Hardly ever = almost never is built step by step (every day? every week? two or three times a year). Facts are right (desert under about 250 mm a year, Antarctica the largest desert). The JA hint is the allowed one. seg7 says "Fen laughs." although Birimo is playing Fen in seg6 (single flag; the same pattern recurs, see block-wide).
- C: Reads well. "Desert" is always the noun (DE-sert). Years and numbers are spelled out.
- Decision: no change.

#### L932 Hot Days, Cold Nights
- A: The diary ("My water bottle felt like hot tea!") is lovely. "Add e, r to geography" gives geographyer; a teacher would say the y comes off. seg12 "Night time." is usually one word in US English.
- B: Same phonics flag (objective spelling rule; the tipJa geography＋er has it too, while the geologist line correctly drops the y). The clouds-as-a-blanket explanation is right. but joins two different things is clear.
- C: "E r and i s t mean a person" is a letter spell-out (house keeper). Reads well otherwise.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Take the y off geography and add e, r. It means a person who studies geography." and tipJa -> geography の y を とって er で geographer (objective). seg12 (say, subtitleEn) "Night time." -> "Nighttime." (objective, US spelling).

#### L933 Sand, Rocks and Oases
- A: The rocky walk, the oasis sign ("Every drop is a gift.") and date palms are natural. seg9 "Jee oh." is not something a teacher writes or says as a sentence.
- B: a place where is modeled with park / kitchen / oasis (good). In seg7 Riko asks and "Fen" answers (as in the teacherScript), while the conversation has Haru ask and Birimo answer; harmless (single flag, kept). Geoglyph = Nazca Lines and Japan's geoparks are right.
- C: "Jee oh" is an invented respelling; TTS may read "Jee" oddly. The words themselves model the sound.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) drop "Jee oh." and JA ジオ。 (A+C).

#### L934 Climates Around the World
- A: The climate map key and the fact-or-opinion aside are good. "Add i, c, and geography becomes a describing word" again skips the dropped y.
- B: Same flag (objective; the tipJa geography → geographic is fine). Climate = usual weather over many years is right; Amazon the largest rainforest, Gobi cold winters right. seg9 "I c makes a describing word" is a spell-out (keeper).
- C: Reads well; "Roll, roll, roll!" is fun.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Take the y off geography and add i, c. Now it's a describing word." (objective).

#### L935 Climate Report
- A: Report Day and the first crystal are well paced. seg3 "The Sahara is in the north of it." is awkward.
- B: located is introduced here (tested) and explained simply. Facts right (Sahara largest hot desert, almost as big as the US; Atacama in Chile; Gobi in Asia; Antarctica largest). seg17 mini report about Japan is a good model.
- C: seg3 ends on a weak "of it" and stumbles.
- Decision: seg3 (say, subtitleEn) -> "The Sahara is in North Africa." (A+C); the JA already says アフリカの きたの ほう.

#### L936 Camels Are Amazing
- A: Humphrey ("Good morning, little travelers" is inside the story) and the fact card are charming. seg7 "No, no. Our humps store fat, not water." has no speaker after Riko's question.
- B: Camel facts are right (back legs first, a week or more, about 100 L, fat not water, eyelashes, nostrils, wide feet). "nay to na" teaches the wrong vowel: natural / national have the short a of cat, not "nah". The caravan JA たいしょう in hiragana reads as 大将 / 対象 for a child.
- C: TTS reads "na" as "nah". The seg7 line runs straight into the answer.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg9 (say, subtitleEn, JA) -> "nay becomes a short a, like in cat ... The short a again" (B+C). seg7 add "Birimo laughs." with JA (A+C). vocab[2].ja -> キャラバン（ラクダと いっしょに たびを する 人たちの れつ） (objective JA).

#### L937 So That They Can Survive
- A: "My ears are my air conditioners!" and the animal notebook are great. "So that means for this reason" is wrong: "for this reason" means "therefore".
- B: So that + can is the target; it tells purpose (what the ears are for), not cause. seg8 "So that tells the reason." has the same slip (JA りゆう). Fennec facts are right (smallest fox, ears release heat, furry feet, burrow by day).
- C: Reads well; "Zip, zip!" and "No burned toes!" are fun.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "So that（～できるように） tells us what something is for." (avoids "in order to" before L939) and seg8 -> "So that tells us why." with JA なんの ためかを おしえるよ (A+B). Lesson is 239 s.

#### L938 Cactus Secrets
- A: The cactus garden, saguaro sign and "a water bottle with spines" are natural.
- B: Spines = special leaves, stem stores water, waxy skin, wide shallow roots are right; saguaro over 12 m and 150+ years is fine. seg8 "To plus a verb gives the reason" (purpose would be more exact) is the usual child-friendly "tells why" (single flag, kept). The tipJa e-drop rule for globe / culture is right.
- C: Reads well. Saguaro is a real word TTS knows.
- Decision: no change.

#### L939 Animals in Cold Places
- A: The compass picture and "We're cousins with opposite ears!" are lovely. seg9 "Say men strongly!" sounds like the word men.
- B: in order to = to, a little more formal, is right and first appears here (tested). Facts right (packed feathers and fat, huddling, polar bear black skin, Arctic fox white in winter). seg19 "Opposite homes, opposite ears!" after a penguin quiz is a little off-topic (single flag, kept).
- C: Same seg9 flag; the hyphen beat form shows the stress.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Say it strong in the middle: en-vi-ron-MEN-tal!" (B+C); JA「メン」を つよく いおう！ already matches.

#### L940 Adaptation Fair
- A: The fair, the hidden poster and Humphrey's surprise are fun. In the teacherScript "It's Humphrey! Yes, it's me!" has no speaker tag.
- B: Adaptation = body parts and habits is right; the poster repeats the L936 facts consistently. The neutral starter works.
- C: Same flag; the reader can't tell the narrator from Humphrey.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "It's Humphrey! He says, Yes, it's me!" (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Suffix rules that skip the dropped y / e**: grep W32 phonics lines for `Add [a-z], [a-z]` and check the base word (L932, L934 fixed; L937 center -> central and L938 / L940 globe -> global are right).
- **Invented respellings**: L933 "Jee oh", L936 "na" fixed. Grep the 60 lessons for short respellings in say ("nah", "na", "jee", "oh" after a root) and for "Say <syllable> strongly" (L939); use the hyphen beat form or "short a, like in cat".
- **"Fen laughs / Fen says / Fen nods / Fen wiggles a paw" in segments right after "Birimo says, I'm playing Fen!"** (L931 seg7, L933 seg7, L934 seg7, L937 seg7, L938 seg7): the narration names the character Birimo is playing. Kept as a single flag (it reads as pretend play), but if you want strict consistency, use "Birimo laughs" etc.
- **so that / to explained as "the reason"**: fixed in L937; L938 / L939 seg8 "To plus a verb gives the reason" kept (child-friendly). Worth one consistent wording ("tells us why") across L937-L940 if you touch them.
- Kept on purpose: letter spell-outs ("a l", "E r and i s t", "I c"), Humphrey's "Good morning" inside the story, L933 seg7 Riko / conversation Haru, L938 seg8 wording, L939 seg19 "Opposite homes, opposite ears!", "Bye-bye!", "Well explained!".

### Run 9 review notes: L941-950 (world 32, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 27 patches in 7 lessons; L942, L944 and L947 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "located" first appears in L942 (after L935), "the graph shows" in L946, "increased / decreased" in L947, "twice as" in L948 and "percent" in L949; each start lesson still has its form. Themes: (1) **Facts**: L943 said Japan is "to the east of Asia" (Japan is part of Asia; now "in East Asia", teacherScript, conversation, seg7). Every graph and table was checked: L948 (60 + 40 + 20 = 120, half goats, a third camels, twice as many camels as horses, a quarter of 40 camels = 10 babies) and L950 (January 25 mm the tallest, about 100 mm a year, less than 250 mm) all add up; L946 every day over 40 degrees with the coolest day still over 40 is consistent; L947 250,000 -> 1,000,000 with a stormy-year dip matches "bars get taller". Continents, oceans, Pacific bigger than all the land, Antarctica the largest desert, Sahara the largest hot desert, Brazil / Amazon, Nazca Lines in Peru, Egypt / Nile, 70% water, about 3% fresh water and most of it ice, about a third of the land desert are all right. (2) **JA たいよう for ocean** (L943 seg3, 7, 16, 19, 20): it sounds like 太陽 (sun) in a desert-sun world; changed to 大きな うみ, which the expression JA and titleJa already use. (3) **Sound spell-outs**: "suh thern" (L941 seg10, L945 seg9) -> "SUH-thern" (the teacherScript form); L950 seg11 "e r n" / "a l" without commas, and "The first sound changes!" for nation -> national (the n does not change; the a does). (4) **Small wording**: L943 "Ward means toward that way", L946 "meters above the sea", L948 "two same parts", L949 "The little dot is point", L945 JA ゆうしん (行進 is こうしん). Every new sentence has 7 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. Touched lessons are 208-231 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L941 North, South, East, West
- A: The sunrise camp, Fen's map notes ("Don't climb them in the afternoon, because the rocks get very hot") and the room model "my bed is to the west of the window" are natural. "Good morning, explorers!" in the teacherScript is Fen's line inside the story (kept).
- B: to the east of, the needle points north and the sun rising in the east are all correct and clearly linked ("the sun can be your compass"). The -ern tip (サザン, not サウザン) is right. seg10 "Not south ern. Say suh thern!" does not match the teacherScript's "SUH-thern, not south-ern".
- C: "suh thern" as two words is an invented respelling; TTS may read "suh" as a sound effect and "thern" oddly. "leads to the western edge" reads fine (verb lead).
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Not south-ern. Say SUH-thern!" (B+C).

#### L942 It's Located in the North
- A: The tent-floor map, the snow-melt river and "Hardly anyone lives there, but the stars at night are amazing" are natural. "Those are the biggest sand dunes, says Fen." uses an inverted tag (fine in narration).
- B: located in the north / in the eastern part of / in the southwest are clear; the north-or-south-first rule and 日本語の ほくとう comparison are right. seg1, seg6, seg14 JA say 山なみ for "the mountains" (the expression JA says 山); close enough (the mountains form a range), single flag, kept.
- C: "Make the second part strong." matches the US stress north-EAST. "Never east north!" reads fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### L943 Continents and Oceans
- A: The giant globe ("It's as tall as Birimo!") and the guidebook are lovely. "Japan... a small island country to the east of Asia" and Riko's "Japan is right here, to the east of Asia" put Japan outside Asia. seg9 "Ward means toward that way." is redundant.
- B: Same fact flag (Japan is in East Asia; it is east of the Asian mainland, but the line says "Asia"). seven continents, five oceans, Pacific bigger than all land, Antarctica the largest desert, Sahara the largest hot desert are right. JA uses たいよう for ocean five times (seg3, 7, 16, 19, 20), which a child hears as 太陽 (sun), especially with the Sun Compass in this world. Same seg9 flag.
- C: "Ward means toward that way" trips; "toward" alone is cleaner. "W, a, r, d" spell-outs read well.
- Decision: "to the east of Asia" -> "in East Asia" in teacherScript, conversation line 3 and seg7 (say, subtitleEn, slide item) with JA アジアの ひがしの ほう (objective, fact). たいよう -> 大きな うみ in the five subtitleJa (objective JA). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Ward means toward." (A+B).

#### L944 Where in the World?
- A: The quiz show with bow tie and bell, Brazil / Amazon, the Nazca Lines card and Egypt / Nile are natural and true. "There is a monkey, a hummingbird, and a spider." is OK in speech (single flag, kept).
- B: It's a country in ~ / Is it ~? / Where is ~? are clear. seg6-7 say "Fen says" for lines Birimo speaks as Fen; in role it reads fine (single flag, kept). goalJa keeps ほんとうの. seg17 "an island country in Asia" agrees with the L943 fix.
- C: "Ding, ding!" and "Boing, boing!" are fine. "ward" as a bare word reads as the real word.
- Decision: no change.

#### L945 Map Guide
- A: The treasure map in a clay jar, the rock like a sleeping camel and the well between two hills are good. Directions are consistent (walk west, then turn north = turn right, as the TPR action shows). Third crystal is right.
- B: Go north for ~ / Turn north at ~ / to the north of ~ are clear. JA seg11 and seg13 write 行進 as ゆうしん (it is こうしん). seg9 "suh thern" again.
- C: "Remember, suh thern." has the respelling problem; the teacherScript already says "Remember, SUH-thern." "Coast and al" and "Geo means earth" read acceptably (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Remember, SUH-thern." (B+C). seg11, seg13 subtitleJa ゆうしん -> こうしん (objective JA).

#### L946 The Graph Shows…
- A: Dr. Mira's arrival and the July notes are natural and consistent (every day over 40, coolest day cloudy). "Dr. Mira's station is three hundred fifty meters above the sea." is not how heights are given (above sea level). "wind cup" is a homemade name for anemometer cups (single flag, kept).
- B: Same "above the sea" flag; a child may picture a station over the sea. The graph shows ~ appears first here (tested). Big-number reading and "no s on hundred / thousand" are right.
- C: Digit spell-outs ("One, two, comma, zero, zero, zero") read well. "read her weather report every month" is present tense and TTS reads it right.
- Decision: teacherScript "above the sea" -> "above sea level" (A+B).

#### L947 It Increased
- A: The park gate line, the laptop bar graph and the camel video are natural. US "waiting in line".
- B: increased / decreased / stayed the same are clear and first appear here (tested); the report numbers (250,000 -> 1,000,000, dip in the stormy year, 50 camels) match every line. "so that the desert plants stay safe" is fine after L937.
- C: Reads well; "One, and then six zeros" is clear.
- Decision: no change.

#### L948 Twice as Many
- A: The oasis table and Dr. Mira's Monday notebook are natural; all the math checks. seg5 "Cut an apple into two same parts." is not natural (two equal parts).
- B: Same seg5 flag; a half needs equal parts, which is the key idea. twice as many ~ as ~ first appears here (tested); "the bigger group comes first" is right for twice as many. The fraction plural rule is right. seg0 "This morning" is story time (kept).
- C: "One, slash, two" reads fine.
- Decision: seg5 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Cut an apple into two equal parts." (A+B).

#### L949 Half, a Quarter, Thirty Percent
- A: The pizza-like pie chart and the water poster are good; 30% desert ("about one third", including cold deserts), 70% water and about 3% fresh water are right. "most of that is frozen ice" is a little redundant but kid-friendly (single flag, kept). seg11 "The little dot is point." is unclear English.
- B: percent appears first here (tested); "Percent means out of one hundred" is clear. Decimals: "We say point for the dot" in the teacherScript is the clear version.
- C: seg11 "The little dot is point." sounds clipped; TTS reads "point" as a verb-like word in isolation.
- Decision: seg11 (say, subtitleEn) -> "We say point for the little dot." (A+C).

#### L950 Graph Presentation
- A: Presentation Day with Fen in the front row and the fourth crystal is a good review. Kiko's rain notes add up (January 25 mm, about 100 mm a year, under 250 mm = desert). "it hardly ever rains in summer" vs "no rain at all" in June to August is fine (general vs this year). seg6 "Haru goes first. The graph shows..." has no "says", but "goes first" marks it (kept).
- B: seg11 "The first sound changes!" is wrong: nation -> national keeps the n; the a changes (ネイ -> ナ, as tipJa says).
- C: seg11 "e r n" and "a l" without commas may be read as words; the teacherScript writes "e, r, n" and "a, l".
- Decision: seg11 (say, subtitleEn) -> "West plus e, r, n. ... Nation plus a, l. ... The first a sounds different!" with JA さいしょの a の おとが かわるよ (objective).

#### Block-wide notes
- **JA たいよう for ocean**: in W32 (Sun Compass, desert sun) たいよう for "ocean" is easy to hear as 太陽. Grep L931-960 subtitleJa / ja for `たいよう` not followed by `の コンパス|の クリスタル|が|は のぼ` etc. and use 大きな うみ (or たいへいよう for the Pacific).
- **Respelled sounds**: "suh thern" (L941, L945) -> "SUH-thern". Grep the block for `suh thern` and for letter groups written without commas (`plus [a-z] [a-z]`), e.g. "e r n", "a l".
- **Japan "to the east of Asia"**: check other lessons (L944 says "an island country in Asia", fine).
- Kept on purpose: L941 Fen's "Good morning, explorers!" (story); L942 山なみ JA and "says Fen" tag; L944 "Fen says" for Birimo-as-Fen, "There is a monkey, ..."; L945 "Coast and al", "Geo means earth"; L946 "wind cup"; L948 "This morning" (story); L949 "frozen ice", "very, very special"; L950 untagged "Haru goes first. The graph shows..."; all "…" in titles and slides, "Bye-bye!", "Bravo!".

### Run 9 review notes: L951-960 (world 32, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 61 patches across all 10 lessons, so every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "because of" first appears in L952 and "as a result" in L953, and neither shows up earlier in this group. Themes: (1) **Invented sound spellings in the data-spelling phonics** (L951-960): "idge", "cher, zher, sher", "eess", bare "al" / "ern". TTS reads "cher" like the singer's name and "al" like the name Al, and the brief asks for real words. Each is now "ends like" a real word: bridge (-age), picture / treasure / washer (-ure), geese (-ease), animal (-al), pattern (-ern), count (-ount, L955 review only). The JA subtitles add the same model word (picture と おなじ「チャー」). (2) **Letter spell-outs without commas** in the review lessons (L958-960): "plus a l" is read as "plus an L", and "w a r d" or "e r n" may be read as a word. They now have commas, like the teacherScripts. In L958, "E a s e, ease" was also wrong because the word ease has a z sound. (3) **Missing speaker tags**: in L952 seg7 Riko seemed to answer her own question, L959 seg7's first quote had no speaker, and in the L959 teacherScript Humphrey's question had no "He asks". (4) **Story and wording fixes**: in the L956 teacherScript, "Ms. Emma opens the book and says" was missed by the I-say pass and also clashed with seg0, where Fen opens the book. L958's speed TPR "Line up!" means "stand in a line". Also fixed: L951's definition "let's all stop, don't do it", L954's "Say about when...", L955's "with a shower one minute shorter" and "We present", L956's "Lina's village was eastern", L959's "How hot asks how much heat", and L953's tipJa とき はなつ. Every fact and every number was checked and is right: Japan 200 L a day = 100 two-liter bottles, 12 L a minute for a shower, 6 L in 30 s = 3 bottles, 10 minutes = 120 L, a newer toilet 5 L a flush, Antarctica, the Sahara almost as big as the US and mostly rock and gravel, the Atacama, Nazca in Peru, the Pacific, the Great Green Wall in the Sahel, camels' humps store fat, fennec ears, the cactus stem, 75% = three quarters. The check script passes with ALL OK. All lessons are 219-229 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L951 Water Is Precious
- A: The Palm Village well, the line of people with buckets and the well sign are natural. seg8 "Let's not means, let's all stop, don't do it." is a muddled definition ("stop" implies we are already doing it). seg17 "Say it your way. My friends are precious to me." is fine as a teacher's own model.
- B: precious, hardly ever (from L931) and let's not are clear. Fen's sign uses "so that" (from L937, fine). The -age = /ɪdʒ/ tip is right for all four words. Same seg8 flag. "Is this a fact or an opinion? Water is precious. It's an opinion..." is debatable but it is framed kindly (single flag, kept).
- C: "idge" in the teacherScript and seg9 is an invented spelling; it is the weakest of the block's respellings, but the review lessons use it too. "Close the lid" (verb) reads right.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Let's not means, let's all choose not to do it." (A+B). teacherScript and seg9 "idge" -> "the end of bridge" (B+C).

#### L952 Because of the Heat
- A: Mirror Lake, the old water line and Dr. Mira's notebook are good. seg7 "Riko asks, Why else did the lake get smaller? Because it hardly ever rained this year." has no speaker for the answer, so Riko seems to answer herself.
- B: because of + noun vs. because + sentence is taught clearly (seg8 and the quiz). The notebook numbers (2 km -> under 1 km) and the three reasons match the questions. "vapor" is US spelling. Same seg7 flag.
- C: "cher, zher, or sher" are invented spellings; TTS reads "cher" like the singer's name (/ʃɛər/) and "zher" is unpredictable.
- Decision: seg7 adds "Birimo says," and JA ビリモ「 (A+C). teacherScript, seg9 and seg10 now use real words: picture, treasure, washer (seg10 "Moisture ends like picture, too."), and the JA adds picture と おなじ「チャー」 etc. (B+C).

#### L953 As a Result
- A: Green Valley and its story board are lovely. The Great Green Wall line is true and kid-friendly (23 words, a bit long, but it is in the reading part; kept).
- B: As a result is introduced here (tested) and is kept apart from so. tipJa "release（はなす・とき はなつ）" has a space inside ときはなつ, so it reads as "time / release". The in- (なかへ) etymology is loose but harmless (single flag, kept).
- C: "eess" (teacherScript, seg9) is an invented spelling; TTS may read it as "ees" or spell it out.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg9 -> "sounds like the end of geese" (B+C). tipJa -> ときはなつ (objective JA).

#### L954 How Much Water Do We Use?
- A: The water chart is clear and every number checks out: 200 L = 100 two-liter bottles, 12 L a minute, 5 minutes = 60 L, 5 flushes x 5 L = 25 L, 30 s = 6 L = 3 bottles, 10 minutes = 120 L. seg7 "Say about when the number is close" reads as "talk about when".
- B: Same seg7 flag; the point is that the word "about" marks a rough number. "toilet" is the fixture (keeper). "account = a report of what happened" is right, if a little advanced. goalJa keeps ほんとうの.
- C: "Say about when..." misleads the reader too. "ount" and "close for nt" (seg8) are borderline, but TTS reads "ount" like count without the c (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Use the word about when the number is close, but not exact." (A+B+C).

#### L955 Save Water Campaign
- A: The poster and the fifth crystal are good. The teacherScript question "How much water can you save with a shower one minute shorter?" is awkward. seg0 "Today is a big day. We present our..." should be progressive for today's event.
- B: by + -ing is clear (point, quiz with the "turn off" distractor). The 12 L and 6 L figures match L954. "cut our water use by one quarter" fits the 1/4 review.
- C: The review strings "Average, idge. Temperature, cher. Increase, eess. Amount, ount." (seg9 and teacherScript) are a run of invented spellings. "We present" also risks the noun stress PRE-sent.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "...if your shower is one minute shorter?" plus the review "Average ends like bridge. Temperature ends like picture. Increase ends like geese. Amount ends like count." (A+B+C). seg9 gets the same (JA with bridge / picture / geese / count と おなじ) (B+C). seg0 -> "We're presenting..." (A+C).

#### L956 A Desert Story
- A: Lina's story by the campfire is warm, and "made-up" is in the teacherScript (tested). In the teacherScript Fen pulls out the book, and then "Ms. Emma opens the book and says"; seg0 says "Fen opens a small, sandy book." seg9 "Lina's village was eastern." is unnatural.
- B: The I-say rule applies to Ms. Emma's own narration (this one was missed). The story says "in the eastern part of a dry valley", so seg9 should match. The relative clause in "a girl who found water" is fine in W32.
- C: Bare "al" and "ern" in seg9 and "Moisture, cher." in seg10.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Fen opens the book, and I say, Read the story..." (objective). seg9 -> "Original ends like animal. Eastern ends like pattern. Lina's village was in the eastern part of the valley." with JA to match (A+B+C). seg10 -> "Moisture ends like picture." (B+C).

#### L957 Geography Quiz Rally
- A: The rally card is fun and every fact is right (Antarctica, the Sahara almost as big as the US and mostly rock and gravel, the Atacama in Chile, the Nazca Lines in Peru, the Pacific).
- B: almost as big as is explained in seg7. tipJa (geoglyph, twelve thousand with no s) is right.
- C: seg9 "Tropical, al." (TTS reads it like the name Al) and seg10 "Shortage, idge."
- Decision: seg9 -> "Tropical ends like animal." and seg10 -> "Shortage ends like bridge.", with JA to match (B+C).

#### L958 My Graph Report
- A: The model report is good. The seg14 speed TPR "Line up!" means "stand in a line"; the move is drawing a line going up.
- B: Same flag. 75% = three quarters is right. seg11 "E a s e, ease." is wrong: the word ease has a z sound, but -ease in decrease has an s sound. The model report's "after people dug a new well" is a new detail but does not contradict L953 (kept).
- C: seg10 "Environment plus a l" is read as "plus an L", and "w a r d" without commas may be read as a word.
- Decision: seg14 "Line up!" (twice) -> "Up, up, up!" (A+B+C). seg10 -> "a, l" and "w, a, r, d" (B+C). seg11 -> "E, a, s, e, like the end of geese." (objective).

#### L959 Desert of the Sun Review
- A: The farewell caravan, Kiko's journal and the geode are lovely. In the teacherScript Humphrey's question ("winks. Do camels store water...?") has no speaker tag. seg7 starts with Kiko's line but no "Kiko says,".
- B: Camels store fat (true). Fennec ears and the cactus stem are right. "Camels have humps so that they can live without food" overstates the fact (they go a long time without food), but it is the pinned core (test regex `so that .*The graph shows`), so I report it and do not change it. seg9 "How hot asks how much heat." is wrong, because "how hot" in "The graph shows how hot it is" is not a question.
- C: seg10 "Coast plus a l." and seg11 "O u n t." need commas.
- Decision: teacherScript "He asks, Do camels..." (A+C). seg7 adds "Kiko says," and JA キコちゃん (A+C). seg9 -> "How hot tells us the temperature." (A+B). seg10 / seg11 commas (B+C).

#### L960 World 32 Grand Review
- A: The festival, the sixth crystal, the needle pointing to the sea and the Island of Fire are all in place (tested). The speed TPR is unchanged.
- B: its own is explained. three million with no s is right. Fact or opinion is fine.
- C: seg10 "add a l" is read as "add an L"; "e r n" and "A g e" need commas; "idge" in the teacherScript and seg11.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg11 -> "A, g, e sounds like the end of bridge." (B+C). seg10 -> "add a, l" and "North plus e, r, n." (B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Invented sound spellings in phonics `say`**: W32 uses "idge / cher / zher / sher / eess / ount / al / ern" as sound labels. "cher" (read like the singer's name) and bare "al" (read like the name Al) are real TTS risks. I used "ends like <real word>": bridge, picture, treasure, washer, geese, animal, pattern, count. Please grep L931-950 (the -al lessons L936-939, -ern / -ward L941-944) for `, al\.|, ern\.|cher|eess|idge` in say / subtitleEn and use the same words, so the review lessons match.
- **Letters without commas**: "plus a l" is read as "plus an L". Grep say for ` a l\b`, `[a-z] [a-z] [a-z] [a-z]\.` (for example "w a r d", "O u n t", "E a s e") and add commas, as the teacherScripts already do.
- **"tap"**: vocab (L954), TPR (L954, L955) and the L954 / L955 cores use "tap"; "faucet" is the usual US word for the fixture ("tap water" is fine). It is a learning item in pinned cores (L955 regex `by [a-z]+ing`, the L954 expression), so I left it. Decide block-wide whether to switch to "faucet" (it would touch the L954 vocab, TPR, expressions, aiTask targets, slides and teacherScripts in L954, 955, 959).
- **L959 core "so that they can live without food"**: slightly overstated (they can go a long time without food). Possible fix: "so that they can go a long time without food" (still matches the test regex). Not changed because it is a core expression.
- Kept on purpose: L951 "fact or opinion" on "Water is precious", the "Say it your way. <model>" seg17 openers (fine as teacher models), L953's long Great Green Wall sentence, the L954 "toilet" fixture and "ount", Celsius "Forty degrees" (L959), L958's "new well" detail, all "Tonight" story-time lines in L956, "Bye-bye!" and "Well done!".

| 901 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, The future is something that we create together. Then the clock door under the square opened. Today, we walk down some old stone stairs. Tick, tock. Tick, tock. At the bottom, there is a very long hallway. It is full of doors. Each door has a different picture on it. Then a tall grandfather clock walks toward us! His pendulum swings from side to side, and his face is round and kind. He bows and says, Welcome to the Corridor of Time! I'm Old Tock, the keeper of the corridor. Each door opens to a different time. Say it with me. Each door opens to a different time. Great! Old Tock gives us an old paper map. It's the Time Map! There are six empty holes on it, and they are shaped like gears. Let's read the note on the back of the map. "The Time Map. This map belongs to brave travelers who want to learn about the past. Open the doors, listen to the people, and ask good questions. Find out how people lived, worked, and played long ago. When you finish each part of your journey, a golden gear will click into one of the six holes. When all six gears shine, the last door will open. Take your time, and enjoy every door. Old Tock, Keeper of the Corridor." How many holes are there on the map? Six. What happens when all six gears shine? The last door will open. Now, phonics. Today, we change a verb into a noun. Add t, i, o, n, and say shun. Invent, invention. Protect, protection. Collect, collection. Connect, connection. This corridor is a connection between now and long ago. | 910 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, This pot was made by people long ago. Today is a special day. Old Tock opens a door with a big golden sign. It says History Museum. Old Tock says, Today, you are the guides! Many visitors are coming. Each friend stands next to one thing. Haru guides the castle model. Kiko guides the old light bulbs. Birimo guides a piece of the Silver Wave. Riko stands next to a black clay pot. She says, Welcome to the History Museum. Please follow me. This was used for cooking about 5,000 years ago. Say it with me. This was used for cooking about 5,000 years ago. Perfect! A little girl raises her hand. How do you know? Riko reads the guide card. Let's read it, too. "Cooking Pot. This pot was found near a river in the 20th century. People long ago used it for cooking soup with fish, nuts, and wild plants. You can still see black marks from the fire on the bottom. Families sat around the fire and shared their food. Our kitchens are very different now, but people still love warm soup and eating together." Where was the pot found? Near a river. What do the black marks show? The pot was used over a fire. Is this a fact or an opinion? People still love warm soup. It's an opinion, but I love it! Then Old Tock chimes. Bong, bong! Click! The second golden gear clicks into the Time Map! Now, phonics. Let's review. Eighteen sixty-eight, 1868. The nineteen nineties, the 1990s. The twentieth century, the 20th century. Educate, education, with t, i, o, n. Entertain, entertainment, with m, e, n, t. A museum gives us education and entertainment! Next time, we will open the Biography Door. | Hello, everyone! Riko stands next to a black clay pot. She says, Welcome to the History Museum. Please follow me. This was used for cooking about 5,000 years ago. Say it with me. | 918 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, When we arrived, the show had already started. Today, we're on time! Old Tock opens a door, and we hear a loud whistle. Choo, choo! It's the railway room. A small steam train runs around a model of two towns and some tall mountains. Professor Nib is sitting on the roof of the train! He waves his tail and says, Long ago, it took a very long time to travel between these two towns. People walked over the mountains. Before the train was built, the trip took ten days. Say it with me. Before the train was built, the trip took ten days. Excellent! Kiko asks, And after the railway opened? Professor Nib says, After the railway opened, the trip took only one day. Haru says, Wow, that's a big change! Let's read the museum card. "Japan's first railway opened in 1872. It ran between Shimbashi and Yokohama. Before the railway, most people walked, and some people rode horses. After the railway opened, travel between the two cities became much faster. At first, some people were surprised by the noisy, smoky steam train. But soon, many people loved riding it. Today, trains carry a huge number of people every day." When did Japan's first railway open? In eighteen seventy-two. How did most people travel before the railway? They walked. Haru says, I think trains are the best invention ever. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Birimo says, I'm playing the steam train! Choo, choo! Now, phonics. Ian means a person who does something. Comedy, comedian. A comedian makes people laugh, just like Birimo. Electric, electrician. An electrician fixes lights and wires. Mathematics, mathematician. Guard, guardian. A guardian takes care of someone. Old Tock is the guardian of the corridor. Next time, let's imagine a world with no electricity. | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, When we arrived, the show had already started.
